  - rationale: We are engaged in a multi-front war against superior forces. Increasing Ranged and Mobile ensures our bazooka and mechanized infantry production is prioritized for defense and counterattacks. Setting UseNuke to 100 guarantees we deploy nuclear weapons as soon as available to cripple The Huns' armies. Reducing Culture and Diplomacy reallocates focus to immediate military needs without compromising essential city-state relations (Monaco, Lisbon). Grand Strategy remains Conquest as nuclear strikes may enable capital captures, though we keep science victory as a backup.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General adjacent to a City and not adjacent to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 1

        - GuardedBias: 8
        - AfraidBias: 1
        - DiplomaticBalance: 8
        - Friendliness: 3
        - WorkWithWillingness: 5
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 9
        - MinorCivWarBias: 7
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 3
        - Rationale: Our multi-front war demands maximum aggression against The Huns and Mongolia, but we must also be wary of overextension due to low happiness. Maintaining current persona values ensures we continue to prioritize military pressure on all enemies while protecting city-state allies like Monaco. No changes needed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Nuclear Fission completes in 2 turns; Advanced Ballistics unlocks Nuclear Missiles and the SS Booster, providing a strategic deterrent against The Huns' overwhelming military and progressing toward a science victory as a backup plan. This aligns with our need for late-game power and nuclear capability.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Media Culture (Policy)
        - Rationale: Media Culture (Freedom Tier 3) provides +1 Happiness per city with a Broadcast Tower (we have them in most cities) and +25% Tourism, helping to alleviate our happiness crisis and resist Mongolia's cultural influence. This is more immediately beneficial than other policies, given our urgent need for happiness and the long-term threat of Mongolia's cultural dominance.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Monaco
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Monaco is a crucial ally (84 influence) with a spy quest against Rome. Our current positive modifier (70) is sufficient to maintain the alliance; resetting to zero allows the in-game AI to reassess based on current actions (like completing the quest) without artificial bias. We want to keep Monaco friendly but not lock the AI into fixed behavior that might conflict with other priorities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Onondaga is our strongest city-state ally (567 influence) and is under naval attack by The Huns. We have already set high positive modifiers; resetting to zero will allow the in-game AI to dynamically respond to the military threat and quest (trade route) without being forced into a fixed posture. Our military actions in the region should naturally protect Onondaga.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Siam is currently at peace with us. They are allied with Rome but weak (military 565). Resetting to zero allows the AI to reassess based on actions; we don't want to lock into hostility unnecessarily while we focus on bigger threats.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: We have just made peace with Arabia (turn 427). Resetting our relationship modifiers to zero allows the in-game AI to reassess diplomatic interactions based on the new peaceful status, rather than being locked into maximum hostility. This avoids artificial bias while we focus on more immediate threats like Mongolia and The Huns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 428
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Huns are the dominant military power, hold three capitals, and have captured our city Warwick. They are actively attacking our vassal The Aztecs. We must keep maximum hostility to focus military efforts on recapturing Warwick, defending Calixtlahuaca, and weakening The Huns' position.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Rome's capital is required for domination victory, and our peace treaty has expired. We need to prepare for war with Rome after dealing with The Huns. Maximum hostility ensures our AI builds forces and plans accordingly.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### England
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: England is a vassal of Mongolia and we are at war with them. We must continue pressing our military advantage to capture Hastings and Nottingham, denying Mongolia a key ally and gaining strategic ground. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI prioritizes their conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Mongolia is our top cultural and diplomatic rival, influencing three civilizations and holding 38 delegates (90% toward Diplomatic Victory). They are a long-term existential threat, especially with their Apollo Program complete. They are also allied with our enemy The Huns indirectly (vassalizing England). Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI prepares for eventual conflict after peace treaties expire and counters their cultural/diplomatic push.
        - UpdatedTurn: 478
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Aztecs have betrayed us and opened a second front near Gaziantep and Tlaxcala. They must be treated as a primary enemy alongside The Huns and Mongolia. Maximum hostility ensures our AI focuses on defeating their units and defending our cities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 482
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Our multi-front war demands maximum military focus, but we must also accelerate great person generation for Monaco and Lisbon quests (Great Musician and Great Writer) to secure city-state alliances. Increased Science, Production, Gold, and TileImprovement are critical to advance our nuclear program, upgrade units, and develop Uranium resources. Reducing Recon, Mobile, and Ranged slightly reallocates focus to these priorities without compromising defense. Grand Strategy remains Conquest as we are at war with all major powers and must capture capitals for domination.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 100
        - Mobilization: 100
        - CityDefense: 100
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 0

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 2 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - World Religion: Confucianism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.

          - Open Door: Budapest:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.

          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 443
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.

            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.

        ### 159
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn
        - NextPlayer: 7: Mongolia
        - Player: 6: The Ottomans
        
        You, Suleiman (leader of The Ottomans, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 485.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General adjacent to a City and not adjacent to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.

      - rationale: With approximately 15 turns until the 500-turn limit, conventional domination is impossible against The Huns' overwhelming military. Our only remaining path to victory—or maximizing final score—is an emergency science push. Switching to Spaceship grand strategy prioritizes Apollo Program and SS part production while keeping strong Defense and Nuke deterrence. We deprioritize Offense, NavalRecon, Wonder, and Culture as conquest and tourism are no longer viable in the remaining turns.
    - #1:

      - args:
        - Policy: Space Procurements
        - Rationale: Space Procurements is essential for a late-game science hail-mary, allowing gold purchase of spaceship parts and faster Spaceship Factory construction. Media Culture's tourism is irrelevant with only ~15 turns left. If we can complete Apollo Program, this tenet could let us rush-buy SS Boosters and other parts for a scientific victory.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 485 (Information Era)
            - We are The Ottomans (Suleiman)
            - At war with: Rome, Mongolia, England, The Huns, The Aztecs
            - Multi-front war situation
            - We have 2 capitals (Istanbul, Tenochtitlan) - 25% towards Domination
            - The Huns have 4 capitals (50%) and are the main contender for Domination
            - Mongolia has 2 capitals (25%) and is at 88% towards Diplomatic Victory with 37 delegates
            - Our happiness is at 36% (Unhappy - combat penalty)
            - Our military strength: 6876 vs The Huns 24473, Mongolia 22278
            - We are technologically behind The Huns (72 techs) and Mongolia (77 techs), we have 66
            - Nuclear Fission completes in 1 turn
            - Next policy in 39 turns
            
            **Key Issues:**
            1. **Critical Happiness Crisis**: 36% happiness is extremely dangerous. We're getting combat penalties and this will cripple our economy and growth.
            2. **Military Overextension**: We're at war with everyone. Our military (6876) is dwarfed by The Huns (24473) and Mongolia (22278).
            3. **Diplomatic Threat**: Mongolia has 37 delegates, needs 42. World Congress voting in 2 turns. They could win Diplomatic Victory soon.
            4. **The Huns Control 4 Capitals**: They are the biggest domination threat.
            5. **War Weariness**: 100% with The Huns, which is devastating.
            
            **Strategic Assessment:**
            We are in a very dangerous position. Being at war with ALL major powers simultaneously while having 36% happiness and 100% war weariness with The Huns is unsustainable.
            
            However, looking at the current flavor settings, they are extremely military-heavy:
            - Offense: 90, Defense: 100, Mobilization: 100, CityDefense: 100, MilitaryTraining: 100
            - Nuke: 90, UseNuke: 80, Naval: 100, Air: 100, Antiair: 100
            - Growth: 90, TileImprovement: 100, Infrastructure: 80, Production: 100, Gold: 100, Science: 100, Culture: 80, Happiness: 100
            
            Wait, I need to re-examine. The current strategy says:
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest
            - Flavors include Happiness: 100, but we're still at 36%
            
            The problem is we have too many 100s. In the flavor system, too many priorities weaken each other. Also, we have 25 turns left on Tlatelolco razing, which is causing -26 happiness delta.
            
            **Tlatelolco Issue**: Tlatelolco is being razed (25 turns left, HappinessDelta: -26). This is a massive happiness drain. We need to stop razing it or finish it quickly. Actually, razing causes unhappiness, and it has 25 turns left. Stopping the raze would immediately remove the razing unhappiness penalty, though we'd keep the city.
            
            Looking at our cities, many have negative happiness deltas:
            - Istanbul: -5
            - Edirne: -3
            - Bursa: -11
            - Konya: -7
            - Samsun: -13
            - Texcoco: -3
            - Byblos: -12
            - Tlatelolco: -26 (razing)
            
            Total from just these: -80 happiness. No wonder we're at 36%.
            
            **Immediate Actions Needed:**
            
            1. **Happiness is existential priority**. We need to stop razing Tlatelolco immediately if possible, or the staff members need to prioritize happiness buildings. With Happiness flavor at 100 already but not working effectively, we need to actually change our approach.
            
            2. **Diplomatically**, we CANNOT continue total war with everyone. We need to make peace with some enemies to focus on the greatest threats. The Aztecs are weak (military 597) but we're at war with them. We have 100% war weariness with The Huns.
            
            But wait - in Civilization V, peace treaties have cooldowns. We don't know the exact peace treaty remaining turns except for Arabia. But we have "Our War Weariness: 100%" with The Huns - this suggests we're desperate for peace but the AI might not offer it.
            
            Actually, looking at the war weariness values:
            - Rome: 0%
            - Mongolia: 0%
            - England: 0%
            - The Huns: 100%
            - The Aztecs: 2%
            
            Wait, The Huns war weariness is 15% according to their view, but our view says 100%. This suggests our people are completely exhausted with The Huns war. 
            
            However, we are set to -100 relationship with all enemies.
            
            **Reality Check**: We are NOT going to win this through conquest right now. We have 6876 military strength vs Huns 24473. We have lost Warwick. We are being pushed back on multiple fronts. Our happiness is crippling. Mongolia is about to win Diplomatic Victory in 2 turns at the World Congress.
            
            Wait - "Voting in 2 turns" for World Congress. The proposal is "Global Hegemony" - which is the Diplomatic Victory proposal. Mongolia has 37 delegates out of 42 needed (88%). If they get a few more votes, they win.
            
            Can we stop Mongolia diplomatically? We only have 4 delegates. We need to somehow steal city-state allies, but most are locked behind Sphere of Influence resolutions.
            
            Actually, looking at the active resolutions:
            - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Segou, Kwa-Bulawayo, Argos, Quebec City
            - Hunnic Sphere of Influence: Ragusa, Stockholm
            
            These are PERMANENT unless repealed. Mongolia has locked in several city-states.
            
            **Can we still stop Mongolia's diplomatic victory?**
            Probably not through normal means. We have 4 delegates. Even if we became allies with some city-states, the Sphere of Influence resolutions make them permanent allies.
            
            **Possible paths to victory:**
            1. **Domination**: Need 8 capitals. Currently have 2. Need 6 more. The Huns have 4, Mongolia 2, Rome 0 (their capital was taken by Huns), etc. We would need to take Rome (held by Huns), Mecca (Huns), Sukhothai (Huns), Attila's Court (Huns), London (Mongolia), Karakorum (Mongolia). We can't do this with our current military.
            
            2. **Science Victory**: We haven't started Apollo Program. Mongolia HAS completed Apollo. The Huns too. We're way behind.
            
            3. **Cultural Victory**: Mongolia is Influential over 4 civs, needs 5. They are at 134% with us. We are Influential over 1 (Aztecs). Mongolia is about to win Cultural too (they need 1 more civ - The Huns at 67%, rising, 35 turns).
            
            4. **Diplomatic Victory**: Impossible, Mongolia has it locked.
            
            5. **Time Victory**: If no one wins by turn 500. Currently turn 485. 15 turns left! Wait, max turns is 500. We are at turn 485. Only 15 turns remaining!
            
            This changes EVERYTHING. With only 15 turns left, we need to maximize score. Mongolia is at 4111, The Huns at 5162, we are at 2817, England at 1535.
            
            Wait, if no one achieves victory by turn 500, highest score wins. But Mongolia is about to get Diplomatic Victory in 2 turns unless stopped. We need to prevent ANY victory for the next 15 turns and have the highest score, OR achieve a victory ourselves.
            
            Actually, with World Congress voting in 2 turns, and Mongolia at 88% (37/42), if the Global Hegemony vote happens and Mongolia gets 42 votes, they win immediately. 
            
            But wait - can we prevent Mongolia from winning? Global Hegemony requires a civ to receive enough support. If no one gets enough, the top two get +5 delegates permanently. We need to ensure Mongolia does NOT get 42 votes.
            
            However, we have no control over World Congress voting directly. But we can try to get more delegates. How?
            - We have 4 delegates. 
            - World Religion: Confucianism gives bonuses to founder/Holy City controller.
            - World Ideology: Order gives bonuses.
            
            Actually, looking at the delegate counts, it seems fixed by city-state allies and resolutions. The Spheres of Influence give permanent delegates.
            
            But Time Victory is in 15 turns. If Mongolia wins Diplomatic in 2 turns, game over. Can we stop them?
            
            Actually, looking more carefully: "Status: World Congress - Voting in 2 turns" - this means we vote in 2 turns. The proposal is Global Hegemony.
            
            Mongolia needs 42 votes. They have 37. If they get 5 more votes from other civs/city-states, they win. 
            
            Wait, in Civ V, you can't vote for yourself in Global Hegemony (actually you can, but you need others to vote for you). Actually in Vox Populi, World Congress delegates vote on Global Hegemony. Each delegate can vote for a civ. The civ with the majority (or required amount) wins.
            
            If Mongolia has 37 of 42 needed, and the proposal passes, do they win? Or do they need 42 votes FOR them? The description says "If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory."
            
            So Mongolia needs 42 delegates voting FOR them. They have 37 delegates themselves. They need 5 more from other civs. Rome has 11, England has 11, The Huns have 22, The Aztecs have 6.
            
            If The Huns (22 votes) vote for Mongolia, Mongolia would get 37+22 = 59, which is more than 42. They win.
            
            But wait - would The Huns vote for Mongolia? They are not friends. They are both powerful. But in the AI logic, they might.
            
            Actually, looking at relationships: The Huns and Mongolia have Open Borders but no declaration of friendship. They might vote for themselves or not vote for Mongolia.
            
            If no one reaches 42, then top two get +5 delegates. But we're almost out of turns.
            
            **Given 15 turns remaining and our desperate situation, what should we do?**
            
            Option A: Try to achieve Science Victory. We need Apollo Program + spaceship parts. We don't have Apollo. Impossible in 15 turns.
            
            Option B: Try to achieve Domination. Impossible in 15 turns with our military.
            
            Option C: Try to achieve Culture. We're influential over 1 civ. Mongolia is influential over 4. Impossible.
            
            Option D: Prevent others from winning and hope for Time Victory. We need to stop Mongolia diplomatically and stop The Huns/Mongolia from other victories.
            
            But if Time Victory happens, highest score wins. The Huns have 5162, Mongolia 4111, we have 2817. We are NOT winning on score.
            
            Wait - unless we can dramatically increase our score in 15 turns. Score is based on cities, population, territory, wonders, techs, military, etc. But catching up 2300+ points in 15 turns is impossible.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider. Are we reading the turn count correctly?
            "MaxTurns: 500"
            Current: Turn 485
            
            But are we SURE it's turn 485 out of 500? Yes, the event log says "Turn 485" and "You are making strategic decisions after turn 485."
            
            If the game ends at turn 500 and no one has won, highest score wins. The Huns will win on score.
            
            Unless... we can somehow win by Domination or another victory condition before turn 500.
            
            Wait. Let's re-examine our situation. We are at war with The Aztecs and have captured their original capital (Tenochtitlan). We are at war with The Huns who took Warwick from us. 
            
            Actually, looking at Domination Victory: "CapitalsNeeded: 8"
            Current: The Huns 4 (50%), Mongolia 2 (25%), Ottomans 2 (25%). Wait, there are 8 capitals total? Let's count:
            1. Rome (held by Huns)
            2. Mecca (held by Huns) 
            3. Sukhothai (held by Huns)
            4. Attila's Court (Huns original)
            5. London (held by Mongolia)
            6. Karakorum (Mongolia original)
            7. Tenochtitlan (held by Ottomans - was Aztec capital)
            8. Istanbul (Ottomans original)
            
            So there are 8 original capitals. We need to control or vassalize all 8. We have 2. The Huns have 4. Mongolia has 2. 
            
            Actually wait - Rome is a civ, their capital is Rome. But Rome is a vassal of The Huns. Does that count for The Huns? The victory progress says The Huns control Rome, Mecca, Sukhothai, Attila's Court. Yes, if Rome is their vassal, they control Rome's capital.
            
            If we took Karakorum and London (Mongolia) and somehow got Rome, Mecca, Sukhothai, Attila's Court... impossible in 15 turns.
            
            **What about the spaceship?** Mongolia has Apollo Program. They could win Science Victory. But they have 0 parts completed. In 15 turns, maybe they can't complete it? Actually with good production they might.
            
            The Huns also have Apollo Program.
            
            **So what can we realistically do in 15 turns?**
            
            1. Our happiness is killing us. We need to fix it.
            2. We can't win militarily against The Huns or Mongolia.
            3. We can't win diplomatically.
            4. We can't win culturally.
            5. We can't win by time (score too low).
            
            Unless... we can somehow dramatically increase our score? No.
            
            Wait, let me reconsider the victory conditions. Is there any way we can prevent a Mongolian diplomatic victory?
            
            Actually, I notice something important: "If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions."
            
            The Huns have 22 delegates. If The Huns vote for themselves, Mongolia might not reach 42. Let's see: Mongolia 37 + possible votes from allies. Rome is a Hun vassal. England is a Mongol vassal. The Aztecs are a Hun vassal.
            
            Rome (11 votes): likely controlled by Huns or voting independently? Rome is a HUN vassal. In Civ V, vassals typically vote with their master. So Rome's 11 votes go to The Huns or whatever The Huns want.
            
            The Aztecs (6 votes): Huns vassal, votes with Huns.
            
            England (11 votes): Mongolia vassal, votes with Mongolia.
            
            So effectively:
            - Mongolia: 37 + 11 (England) = 48 votes if England votes with them. That's enough to win!
            - The Huns: 22 + 11 (Rome) + 6 (Aztecs) = 39.
            
            Wait, but England might vote for Mongolia. If Mongolia gets England's votes, they have 48, which exceeds 42. They win Diplomatic Victory.
            
            Unless... we can somehow break England's vassalage or prevent them from voting? No.
            
            Actually, can we liberate a city-state to break Sphere of Influence? No, those are World Congress resolutions.
            
            Can we capture the United Nations (Karakorum) to stop the vote? Karakorum has defense 122 and is deep in Mongol territory.

            Looking at tactical zones: Enemy Land Zone 2678 (Tlaxcala) shows The Aztecs have units there, and we have posture "Withdraw" with FriendlyStrength 1204 vs Enemy 3608. We're actually losing that fight! Calixtlahuaca (26212) also shows "Withdraw" with us having 1553 vs enemy 4740. The Huns and Aztecs are pushing us back there.
            
            If we peace with Aztecs, The Huns might still attack Calixtlahuaca. But Aztec units would stop. That helps.
            
            But the bigger picture: with 15 turns and multiple massive enemies, does it matter? Yes, every little bit helps. But peacing with Aztecs gives us 20 turns of peace (standard treaty), which extends past turn 500. So it's all upside - we don't need to fight them again before the game ends.
            
            Actually, peace treaties last 10 turns on standard speed. Turn 500 is 15 turns away. So peace would last until turn 495, then we could be at war again for the last 5 turns. Not that it matters.
            
            But freeing up units from the Aztec front could help reinforce against The Huns or recapture Warwick.
            
            Similarly, peace with Rome? Rome is weak (military 4151). But they're far away. We don't seem to have units fighting Rome (no tactical zones near Rome). Peace with Rome would be irrelevant.
            
            Peace with England? We're at war but not actively invading them. Peace might let us focus, but distant.
            
            Peace with Mongolia? They're on our northern/eastern border. We have zone 1077 (Segou) where they are. Peace might help but they're unbeatable.
            
            Peace with The Huns? We have 100% war weariness. We NEED peace with The Huns. But The Huns have 15% war weariness and are winning. They won't peace unless we give them a lot. But maybe raising our relationship would help? No, the tool only affects our AI's attitude. The peace negotiation is bilateral. The Huns' willingness depends on their own assessment.
            
            Actually, `set-relationship` for The Huns is currently -100. If we increase it (make it less negative), our staff members become more willing to accept peace terms. But The Huns might still not offer good terms.
            
            However, with The Huns having Warwick and pushing us, and our 100% war weariness, suing for peace might be necessary to stop the War Weariness penalty. War Weariness 100% causes massive happiness penalties and combat penalties. If we make peace, we lose war weariness over time.
            
            But can we even make peace? The Huns hold our city Warwick. They probably want to keep it or ask for a lot.
            
            If we peace with The Huns, Mongolia, Aztecs, England... we'd be at peace with everyone. Then we can focus on happiness, science, and score. But with 15 turns, can we build up enough to win Time? No.
            
            **But here's the thing**: if Mongolia wins Diplomatic in 2 turns, none of this matters. If they don't, then we have ~13 turns to outscore everyone or achieve another victory.
            
            Given the uncertainty, I think we should prepare for both scenarios:
            1. Assume Mongolia might not win (or the vote fails), in which case we need to maximize our position.
            2. Assume we might need to fight hard for the remaining turns.
            
            **What concrete moves should we make?**
            
            A. **Stop the Tlatelolco raze if possible.** This is the biggest happiness hit. We can't directly stop it, but maybe we can annex the city and stop razing? The staff members might not do this. However, if we emphasize Happiness heavily and maybe Growth/Expansion, they might reconsider. But in Civ V, you can only stop razing by annexing the city (which stops razing immediately and makes it a normal city). If it's already being razed, clicking "Annex" sometimes stops it. But Tlatelolco is already being razed. The AI might not be able to stop it mid-raze.
            
            Actually, looking at the city report for Tlatelolco: "IsOccupied: true" and it's building a Stadium? No, CurrentProduction: Stadium, ProductionTurnsLeft: 37153 - that's a broken number meaning it's not actually producing. It's razing. In Civ V, when razing, you can't build anything. The 37153 turns is a placeholder.
            
            Can we save this city? In most Civ V versions, once razing starts, it continues. So we might be stuck with -26 happiness for 25 turns. That puts us at ~60% happiness when it finishes. But we'll lose before then.
            
            B. **Happiness buildings**: We should emphasize Happiness flavor to 100 (already there) and maybe reduce competing priorities so the AI actually builds happiness buildings. Our cities have Public Works in production in many places. Public Works is a building that gives happiness (I think +1 and +1 specialist slot in Vox Populi?). Actually, Public Works might give +1 happiness, +1 production, etc. Several cities are building it.
            
            C. **Military**: We need to hold the line. Defense: 100, CityDefense: 100. But we also need Offense: 90 or 100 if we want to try quick strikes.
            
            D. **Science**: Keep pushing for nukes.
            
            E. **Gold**: We're at -78 GPT. We have 2119 gold. We should prioritize Gold less and maybe use our gold to buy units or rush buildings.
            
            Actually, looking at our flavors: Gold is 100. That makes the AI prioritize gold. But we're running a deficit. Maybe reduce Gold to 50 and increase something else.
            
            **I think the key insight is**: With 15 turns left and maximum hostility on all sides, our best bet is actually to try for a dramatic military/science play. We should:
            1. Finish nukes ASAP.
            2. Use nukes to break enemy armies or retake Warwick.
            3. Survive.
            
            But we also need to address happiness. The razing of Tlatelolco is killing us. Can we change our annexation/raze policy? There's no tool for that.
            
            Wait, what if we set Expansion to a higher value? Expansion prioritizes settlers, which doesn't help. 
            
            What if we set Growth to 100? Tlatelolco razing is reducing growth potential.
            
            Let me reconsider the flavors. With so many at 100, they're all competing. Let's streamline:
            
            **To win/score best in 15 turns:**
            - Science: 100 (get nukes/SS parts)
            - Production: 100 (build fast)
            - Nuke: 100 (build nukes)
            - UseNuke: 100 (actually use them)
            - Happiness: 100 (fix crisis)
            - CityDefense: 90
            - Defense: 90
            - Offense: 80
            - MilitaryTraining: 80
            - Air: 100 (we have fighters/bombers, need air superiority)
            - Gold: 70
            - Growth: 80
            - TileImprovement: 50
            - Infrastructure: 50
            - GreatPeople: 50
            
            Remove unnecessary priorities like NavalTileImprovement (80), NavalGrowth (80), NavalRecon (100), Espionage (100) - those were for long-term city-state quests which no longer matter.
            
            Actually, Espionage at 100 was for science stealing. We're behind but 15 turns is too short to catch up via theft.
            
            Naval was 100 because we needed naval power. But our navy is getting sunk (destroyer killed by Onondaga/barbarians, another by Huns). We should keep some naval defense but not 100.
            
            Let me look at our naval situation. We have several sea zones. Enemy sea zones show significant strength. But building more ships in 15 turns won't change much.
            
            **Air**: We have fighters and B17s. Air superiority is crucial. Keep Air: 100.
            
            **Mobile**: 60. We need mobile units for rapid response. Maybe increase to 80.
            
            **Airlift**: 0. We should increase this to move troops quickly between theaters. But with only 15 turns, maybe not useful.
            
            **What about the Space strategy?** We don't have Apollo. Forget it.
            
            **What about allying with City-States for score?** Capturing or allying with city-states gives score. But our Diplomacy flavor is 80. We could use our remaining gold to buy influence. But Sphere of Influence resolutions block many.
            
            We have 2119 gold. Influence costs increase over time. Monaco needs ~800 to be ally? We have 290 influence, need maybe 500 more. At ~50 gold per influence... actually, in Vox Populi, you can gift gold directly or complete quests. There's a quest for Great Musician (30 turns). Too slow.
            
            Budapest: our 452 influence. The Huns have 556. We'd need ~200+ to overtake. That takes a lot of gold. Not worth it for 2 delegates.
            
            Lisbon: 284, Mongolia 516. Need 300+. Not worth it.
            
            Onondaga: -60. Mongolia 989. Impossible.
            
            So city-state diplomacy is dead end.
            
            **Final Strategic Position:**
            
            The situation is dire. Mongolia likely wins Diplomatic Victory in 2 turns. If not, The Huns probably win by score at turn 500 or by Domination/Science eventually. We are significantly behind.
            
            BUT, the only way to play is to fight for every advantage:
            1. **Recapture Warwick and hold Calixtlahuaca** - these are our cities. Losing them hurts score and territory.
            2. **Develop nukes** - Advanced Ballistics next. If the game continues, nukes let us reclaim cities or damage enemies.
            3. **Fix happiness** - avoid further penalties. Though Tlatelolco razing is unavoidable.
            4. **Consolidate military** - focus on key fronts rather than spreading thin.
            
            The Aztec front is currently losing (Withdraw posture at Tlaxcala and Calixtlahuaca). But Calixtlahuaca has The Huns, not Aztecs. Wait, Calixtlahuaca (26212) shows The Aztecs AND The Huns. It's complicated.
            
            Looking at Enemy Land Zone 26212 (Calixtlahuaca), the enemy strength is 4740 (Huns + Aztecs), we have 1553. Posture: Withdraw. This means we're about to lose Calixtlahuaca if we don't reinforce.
            
            Warwick (24552) has EnemyStrength 9703. We have no friendly strength there. We've already lost it.
            
            So we should:
            - Abandon Calixtlahuaca? It's a puppet anyway. Losing it is bad but not catastrophic.
            - Focus on defending Gaziantep (9517), our fortress city with 132 defense.
            - Defend Istanbul.
            
            **What changes to make to tools:**
            
            **Research**: Keep Advanced Ballistics. Rationale: If the game continues beyond the World Congress vote, nuclear missiles provide our only realistic path to rapid territorial gains or deterring further aggression. The SS Booster also advances a backup science victory path.
            
            **Policy**: Keep Media Culture. Even though it's 39 turns away, if any event extends the game (e.g., we somehow survive the vote), this policy provides happiness and tourism benefits.
            
            **Persona**: Keep current high aggression but maybe slightly reduce Meanness or Neediness? No, with all-out war, keep it.
            
            **Relationships**: 
            - The Aztecs: Change from -100 to 0. Rationale: The Aztecs are militarily irrelevant (597 strength) but tying down forces on our southern front. With only ~15 turns remaining, peace with The Aztecs allows redirection of units to critical fronts against The Huns and Mongolia, and removes a source of war weariness. They have one city left and pose no existential threat.
            - Actually, should we peace with anyone else? Peace with Rome? We're not fighting them actively. But they're at war. Might as well keep hostility to prevent them from joining assaults. 
            - England: They vote for Mongolia. But we can't do anything. Keep -100.
            - Mongolia: -100. Keep.
            - The Huns: -100. Keep.
            
            Wait, if we peace with Aztecs, our units in that area won't have enemy units to fight. But the Huns are also there. The Aztecs are separate. 
            
            Actually, looking at zone 2678 (Tlaxcala), it's just Aztecs. If we peace, Aztec units become neutral (but we have to withdraw). Then we can move our units north to help against The Huns at Calixtlahuaca or hold Gaziantep.
            
            This seems wise.
            
            For **Flavors/Strategy**:
            - Reduce the number of 100-priority items so they actually take effect.
            - Grand Strategy: Keep Conquest. We're not going to win Culture or Science or UN. Conquest is the only aggressive path.
            - Key flavors:
              - Nuke: 100 (absolute priority)
              - UseNuke: 100 (absolute priority) 
              - Science: 100
              - Production: 100
              - Happiness: 100
              - Defense: 90
              - CityDefense: 90
              - Offense: 80
              - Air: 90 (maintain air control)
              - Mobilization: 90
              - Gold: 50 (accept deficit, focus production)
              - Growth: 70
              - TileImprovement: 40
              - Infrastructure: 40
              - GreatPeople: 40
              - Naval: 50
              - NavalRecon: 30
              - Espionage: 30
              - Culture: 20 (abandon cultural)
              - Diplomacy: 20 (abandon city-state quests)
              - Spaceship: 30 (slight backup)
              - Expansion: 0
              - Recon: 0
            
            Wait, but if we have too many high priorities, they still compete. Let me pick truly essential ones:
            
            - Nuke: 100
            - UseNuke: 100
            - Science: 90
            - Production: 90
            - Happiness: 90
            - Defense: 80
            - CityDefense: 80
            - Offense: 70
            - Air: 80
            - Mobilization: 80
            
            Everything else lower.
            
            Actually, the instructions say: "Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each."
            
            So I should have fewer 100s. Let's do:
            - Nuke: 100
            - UseNuke: 100
            - Science: 80
